<span class="k">func</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="n">Parent2</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="kt">error</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>If a function returns two or more parameters of the same type, adding names can
be useful.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="n">Children</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">left</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">right</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="kt">error</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>If the caller must take action on particular result parameters, naming them can
help suggest what the action is:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// WithTimeout returns a context that will be canceled no later than d duration</span>
<span class="c">// from now.</span>
<span class="c">//</span>
<span class="c">// The caller must arrange for the returned cancel function to be called when</span>
<span class="c">// the context is no longer needed to prevent a resource leak.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">WithTimeout</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parent</span> <span class="n">Context</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span> <span class="n">time</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Duration</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">ctx</span> <span class="n">Context</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">cancel</span> <span class="k">func</span><span class="p">())</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>In the code above, cancellation is a particular action a caller must take.
However, were the result parameters written as <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">(Context, func())</code> alone, it
would be unclear what is meant by “cancel function”.</p>

<p>Don’t use named result parameters when the names produce
<a href="#repetitive-with-type">unnecessary repetition</a>.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="n">Parent1</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">node</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="n">Parent2</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">node</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Node</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="kt">error</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Don’t name result parameters in order to avoid declaring a variable inside the
function. This practice results in unnecessary API verbosity at the cost of
minor implementation brevity.</p>

<p><a href="https://tour.golang.org/basics/7">Naked returns</a> are acceptable only in a small function. Once it’s a
medium-sized function, be explicit with your returned values. Similarly, do not
name result parameters just because it enables you to use naked returns.
<a href="guide#clarity">Clarity</a> is always more important than saving a few lines in
your function.</p>

<p>It is always acceptable to name a result parameter if its value must be changed
in a deferred closure.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p><strong>Tip:</strong> Types can often be clearer than names in function signatures.
<a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/index.html#gotip">GoTip #38: Functions as Named Types</a> demonstrates this.</p>

  <p>In, <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/context#WithTimeout"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">WithTimeout</code></a> above, the real code uses a <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/context#CancelFunc"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">CancelFunc</code></a> instead of a
raw <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">func()</code> in the result parameter list and requires little effort to
document.</p>
</blockquote>

<p><a id="package-comments"></a></p>

<h3 id="package-comments">Package comments</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-PackageComments"></a></p>

<p>Package comments must appear immediately above the package clause with no blank
line between the comment and the package name. Example:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// Package math provides basic constants and mathematical functions.</span>
<span class="c">//</span>
<span class="c">// This package does not guarantee bit-identical results across architectures.</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">math</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>There must be a single package comment per package. If a package is composed of
multiple files, exactly one of the files should have a package comment.</p>

<p>Comments for <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">main</code> packages have a slightly different form, where the name of
the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">go_binary</code> rule in the BUILD file takes the place of the package name.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// The seed_generator command is a utility that generates a Finch seed file</span>
<span class="c">// from a set of JSON study configs.</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">main</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Other styles of comment are fine as long as the name of the binary is exactly as
written in the BUILD file. When the binary name is the first word, capitalizing
it is required even though it does not strictly match the spelling of the
command-line invocation.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// Binary seed_generator ...</span>
<span class="c">// Command seed_generator ...</span>
<span class="c">// Program seed_generator ...</span>
<span class="c">// The seed_generator command ...</span>
<span class="c">// The seed_generator program ...</span>
<span class="c">// Seed_generator ...</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Tips:</p>

<ul>
  <li>
    <p>Example command-line invocations and API usage can be useful documentation.
For Godoc formatting, indent the comment lines containing code.</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>If there is no obvious primary file or if the package comment is
extraordinarily long, it is acceptable to put the doc comment in a file
named <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">doc.go</code> with only the comment and the package clause.</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>Multiline comments can be used instead of multiple single-line comments.
This is primarily useful if the documentation contains sections which may be
useful to copy and paste from the source file, as with sample command-lines
(for binaries) and template examples.</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">/*
The seed_generator command is a utility that generates a Finch seed file
from a set of JSON study configs.

    seed_generator *.json | base64 &gt; finch-seed.base64
*/</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">template</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>Comments intended for maintainers and that apply to the whole file are
typically placed after import declarations. These are not surfaced in Godoc
and are not subject to the rules above on package comments.</p>
  </li>
</ul>

<p><a id="imports"></a></p>

<h2 id="imports">Imports</h2>

<p><a id="TOC-Imports"></a></p>

<p><a id="import-renaming"></a></p>

<h3 id="import-renaming">Import renaming</h3>

<p>Package imports shouldn’t normally be renamed, but there are cases where they
must be renamed or where a rename improves readability.</p>

<p>Local names for imported packages must follow
<a href="#package-names">the guidance around package naming</a>, including the prohibition
on the use of underscores and capital letters. Try to be
<a href="guide#consistency">consistent</a> by always using the same local name for the same
imported package.</p>

<p>An imported package <em>must</em> be renamed to avoid a name collision with other
imports. (A corollary of this is that <a href="#package-names">good package names</a>
should not require renaming.) In the event of a name collision, prefer to rename
the most local or project-specific import.</p>

<p>Generated protocol buffer packages <em>must</em> be renamed to remove underscores from
their names, and their local names must have a <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">pb</code> suffix. See
<a href="best-practices#import-protos">proto and stub best practices</a> for more
information.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">foosvcpb</span> <span class="s">"path/to/package/foo_service_go_proto"</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Lastly, an imported, non-autogenerated package <em>can</em> be renamed if it has an
uninformative name (e.g. <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">util</code> or <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">v1</code>) Do this sparingly: do not rename the
package if the code surrounding the use of the package conveys enough context.
When possible, prefer refactoring the package itself with a more suitable name.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">core</span> <span class="s">"github.com/kubernetes/api/core/v1"</span>
    <span class="n">meta</span> <span class="s">"github.com/kubernetes/apimachinery/pkg/apis/meta/v1beta1"</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>If you need to import a package whose name collides with a common local variable
name that you want to use (e.g. <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">url</code>, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">ssh</code>) and you wish to rename the
package, the preferred way to do so is with the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">pkg</code> suffix (e.g. <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">urlpkg</code>).
Note that it is possible to shadow a package with a local variable; this rename
is only necessary if the package still needs to be used when such a variable is
in scope.</p>

<p><a id="import-grouping"></a></p>

<h3 id="import-grouping">Import grouping</h3>

<p>Imports should be organized into the following groups, in order:</p>

<ol>
  <li>
    <p>Standard library packages</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>Other (project and vendored) packages</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>Protocol Buffer imports (e.g., <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">fpb "path/to/foo_go_proto"</code>)</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>Import for <a href="https://go.dev/doc/effective_go#blank_import">side-effects</a>
(e.g., <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">_ "path/to/package"</code>)</p>
  </li>
</ol>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">main</span>

<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="s">"fmt"</span>
    <span class="s">"hash/adler32"</span>
    <span class="s">"os"</span>

    <span class="s">"github.com/dsnet/compress/flate"</span>
    <span class="s">"golang.org/x/text/encoding"</span>
    <span class="s">"google.golang.org/protobuf/proto"</span>

    <span class="n">foopb</span> <span class="s">"myproj/foo/proto/proto"</span>

    <span class="n">_</span> <span class="s">"myproj/rpc/protocols/dial"</span>
    <span class="n">_</span> <span class="s">"myproj/security/auth/authhooks"</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="import-blank"></a></p>

<h3 id="import-blank-import-_">Import “blank” (<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">import _</code>)</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-ImportBlank"></a></p>

<p>Packages that are imported only for their side effects (using the syntax <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">import
_ "package"</code>) may only be imported in a main package, or in tests that require
them.</p>

<p>Some examples of such packages include:</p>

<ul>
  <li>
    <p><a href="https://pkg.go.dev/time/tzdata">time/tzdata</a></p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><a href="https://pkg.go.dev/image/jpeg">image/jpeg</a> in image processing code</p>
  </li>
</ul>

<p>Avoid blank imports in library packages, even if the library indirectly depends
on them. Constraining side-effect imports to the main package helps control
dependencies, and makes it possible to write tests that rely on a different
import without conflict or wasted build costs.</p>

<p>The following are the only exceptions to this rule:</p>

<ul>
  <li>
    <p>You may use a blank import to bypass the check for disallowed imports in the
<a href="https://github.com/bazelbuild/rules_go/blob/master/go/nogo.rst">nogo static checker</a>.</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>You may use a blank import of the <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/embed">embed</a> package
in a source file which uses the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">//go:embed</code> compiler directive.</p>
  </li>
</ul>

<p><strong>Tip:</strong> If you create a library package that indirectly depends on a
side-effect import in production, document the intended usage.</p>

<p><a id="import-dot"></a></p>

<h3 id="import-dot-import-">Import “dot” (<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">import .</code>)</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-ImportDot"></a></p>

<p>The <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">import .</code> form is a language feature that allows bringing identifiers
exported from another package to the current package without qualification. See
the <a href="https://go.dev/ref/spec#Import_declarations">language spec</a> for more.</p>

<p>Do <strong>not</strong> use this feature in the Google codebase; it makes it harder to tell
where the functionality is coming from.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">foo_test</span>

<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="s">"bar/testutil"</span> <span class="c">// also imports "foo"</span>
    <span class="o">.</span> <span class="s">"foo"</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">myThing</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Bar</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="c">// Bar defined in package foo; no qualification needed.</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">foo_test</span>

<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="s">"bar/testutil"</span> <span class="c">// also imports "foo"</span>
    <span class="s">"foo"</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">myThing</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">foo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Bar</span><span class="p">()</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="errors"></a></p>

<h2 id="errors">Errors</h2>

<p><a id="returning-errors"></a></p>

<h3 id="returning-errors">Returning errors</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-ReturningErrors"></a></p>

<p>Use <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">error</code> to signal that a function can fail. By convention, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">error</code> is the
last result parameter.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">Good</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="kt">error</span> <span class="p">{</span> <span class="c">/* ... */</span> <span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Returning a <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code> error is the idiomatic way to signal a successful operation
that could otherwise fail. If a function returns an error, callers must treat
all non-error return values as unspecified unless explicitly documented
otherwise. Commonly, the non-error return values are their zero values, but this
cannot be assumed.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">GoodLookup</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Result</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="kt">error</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// ...</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="no">nil</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">res</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="no">nil</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Exported functions that return errors should return them using the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">error</code> type.
Concrete error types are susceptible to subtle bugs: a concrete <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code> pointer
can get wrapped into an interface and thus become a non-nil value (see the
<a href="https://golang.org/doc/faq#nil_error">Go FAQ entry on the topic</a>).</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">Bad</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">os</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">PathError</span> <span class="p">{</span> <span class="c">/*...*/</span> <span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><strong>Tip:</strong> A function that takes a <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/context"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">context.Context</code></a> argument should usually
return an <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">error</code> so that the caller can determine if the context was cancelled
while the function was running.</p>

<p><a id="error-strings"></a></p>

<h3 id="error-strings">Error strings</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-ErrorStrings"></a></p>

<p>Error strings should not be capitalized (unless beginning with an exported name,
a proper noun or an acronym) and should not end with punctuation. This is
because error strings usually appear within other context before being printed
to the user.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Something bad happened."</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"something bad happened"</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>On the other hand, the style for the full displayed message (logging, test
failure, API response, or other UI) depends, but should typically be
capitalized.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">log</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Infof</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Operation aborted: %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">log</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Operation aborted: %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Op(%q) failed unexpectedly; err=%v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">args</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="handle-errors"></a></p>

<h3 id="handle-errors">Handle errors</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-HandleErrors"></a></p>

<p>Code that encounters an error should make a deliberate choice about how to
handle it. It is not usually appropriate to discard errors using <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">_</code> variables.
If a function returns an error, do one of the following:</p>

<ul>
  <li>Handle and address the error immediately.</li>
  <li>Return the error to the caller.</li>
  <li>In exceptional situations, call <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/golang/glog#Fatal"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">log.Fatal</code></a> or (if absolutely necessary)
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">panic</code>.</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>Note:</strong> <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">log.Fatalf</code> is not the standard library log. See [#logging].</p>

<p>In the rare circumstance where it is appropriate to ignore or discard an error
(for example a call to <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/bytes#Buffer.Write"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">(*bytes.Buffer).Write</code></a> that is documented to never
fail), an accompanying comment should explain why this is safe.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">b</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">bytes</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Buffer</span>

<span class="n">n</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">_</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">b</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Write</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">p</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="c">// never returns a non-nil error</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>For more discussion and examples of error handling, see
<a href="http://golang.org/doc/effective_go.html#errors">Effective Go</a> and
<a href="/styleguide/go/best-practices.html#error-handling">best practices</a>.</p>

<p><a id="in-band-errors"></a></p>

<h3 id="in-band-errors">In-band errors</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-In-Band-Errors"></a></p>

<p>In C and similar languages, it is common for functions to return values like -1,
null, or the empty string to signal errors or missing results. This is known as
in-band error handling.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="c">// Lookup returns the value for key or -1 if there is no mapping for key.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">Lookup</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">key</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="kt">int</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Failing to check for an in-band error value can lead to bugs and can attribute
errors to the wrong function.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="c">// The following line returns an error that Parse failed for the input value,</span>
<span class="c">// whereas the failure was that there is no mapping for missingKey.</span>
<span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">Parse</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">Lookup</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">missingKey</span><span class="p">))</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Go’s support for multiple return values provides a better solution (see the
<a href="http://golang.org/doc/effective_go.html#multiple-returns">Effective Go section on multiple returns</a>). Instead of requiring clients to
check for an in-band error value, a function should return an additional value
to indicate whether its other return values are valid. This return value may be
an error or a boolean when no explanation is needed, and should be the final
return value.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// Lookup returns the value for key or ok=false if there is no mapping for key.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">Lookup</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">key</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">value</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ok</span> <span class="kt">bool</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>This API prevents the caller from incorrectly writing <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">Parse(Lookup(key))</code> which
causes a compile-time error, since <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">Lookup(key)</code> has 2 outputs.</p>

<p>Returning errors in this way encourages more robust and explicit error handling:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">value</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ok</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Lookup</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">key</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="o">!</span><span class="n">ok</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"no value for %q"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">key</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">Parse</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">value</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Some standard library functions, like those in package <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">strings</code>, return in-band
error values. This greatly simplifies string-manipulation code at the cost of
requiring more diligence from the programmer. In general, Go code in the Google
codebase should return additional values for errors.</p>

<p><a id="indent-error-flow"></a></p>

<h3 id="indent-error-flow">Indent error flow</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-IndentErrorFlow"></a></p>

<p>Handle errors before proceeding with the rest of your code. This improves the
readability of the code by enabling the reader to find the normal path quickly.
This same logic applies to any block which tests a condition then ends in a
terminal condition (e.g., <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">return</code>, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">panic</code>, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">log.Fatal</code>).</p>

<p>Code that runs if the terminal condition is not met should appear after the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">if</code>
block, and should not be indented in an <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">else</code> clause.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// error handling</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="c">// or continue, etc.</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="c">// normal code</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// error handling</span>
<span class="p">}</span> <span class="k">else</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// normal code that looks abnormal due to indentation</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<blockquote>
  <p><strong>Tip:</strong> If you are using a variable for more than a few lines of code, it is
generally not worth using the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">if</code>-with-initializer style. In these cases, it
is usually better to move the declaration out and use a standard <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">if</code>
statement:</p>

  <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">x</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">f</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="c">// error handling</span>
  <span class="k">return</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="c">// lots of code that uses x</span>
<span class="c">// across multiple lines</span>
</code></pre></div>  </div>

  <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">f</span><span class="p">();</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="c">// error handling</span>
  <span class="k">return</span>
<span class="p">}</span> <span class="k">else</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="c">// lots of code that uses x</span>
  <span class="c">// across multiple lines</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div>  </div>
</blockquote>

<p>See <a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/index.html#gotip">Go Tip #1: Line of Sight</a> and
<a href="https://testing.googleblog.com/2017/06/code-health-reduce-nesting-reduce.html">TotT: Reduce Code Complexity by Reducing Nesting</a>
for more details.</p>

<p><a id="language"></a></p>

<h2 id="language">Language</h2>

<p><a id="literal-formatting"></a></p>

<h3 id="literal-formatting">Literal formatting</h3>

<p>Go has an exceptionally powerful <a href="https://golang.org/ref/spec#Composite_literals">composite literal syntax</a>, with which it is
possible to express deeply-nested, complicated values in a single expression.
Where possible, this literal syntax should be used instead of building values
field-by-field. The <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">gofmt</code> formatting for literals is generally quite good, but
there are some additional rules for keeping these literals readable and
maintainable.</p>

<p><a id="literal-field-names"></a></p>

<h4 id="field-names">Field names</h4>

<p>Struct literals must specify <strong>field names</strong> for types defined outside the
current package.</p>

<ul>
  <li>
    <p>Include field names for types from other packages.</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// https://pkg.go.dev/encoding/csv#Reader</span>
<span class="n">r</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">csv</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Reader</span><span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">Comma</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="sc">','</span><span class="p">,</span>
  <span class="n">Comment</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="sc">'#'</span><span class="p">,</span>
  <span class="n">FieldsPerRecord</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">4</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>

    <p>The position of fields in a struct and the full set of fields (both of which
are necessary to get right when field names are omitted) are not usually
considered to be part of a struct’s public API; specifying the field name is
needed to avoid unnecessary coupling.</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">r</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">csv</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Reader</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="sc">','</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="sc">'#'</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">4</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>For package-local types, field names are optional.</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">okay</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="m">42</span><span class="p">}</span>
<span class="n">also</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">internalType</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="m">4</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">2</span><span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>

    <p>Field names should still be used if it makes the code clearer, and it is
very common to do so. For example, a struct with a large number of fields
should almost always be initialized with field names.</p>

    <!-- TODO: Maybe a better example here that doesn't have many fields. -->

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">okay</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">StructWithLotsOfFields</span><span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">field1</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">,</span>
  <span class="n">field2</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"two"</span><span class="p">,</span>
  <span class="n">field3</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">3.14</span><span class="p">,</span>
  <span class="n">field4</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">true</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>
  </li>
</ul>

<p><a id="literal-matching-braces"></a></p>

<h4 id="matching-braces">Matching braces</h4>

<p>The closing half of a brace pair should always appear on a line with the same
amount of indentation as the opening brace. One-line literals necessarily have
this property. When the literal spans multiple lines, maintaining this property
keeps the brace matching for literals the same as brace matching for common Go
syntactic constructs like functions and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">if</code> statements.</p>

<p>The most common mistake in this area is putting the closing brace on the same
line as a value in a multi-line struct literal. In these cases, the line should
end with a comma and the closing brace should appear on the next line.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{{</span><span class="n">Key</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"value"</span><span class="p">}}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">Key</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"multi"</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">Key</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"line"</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">bad</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">Key</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"multi"</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">Key</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"line"</span><span class="p">}}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">bad</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">Key</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"value"</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="literal-cuddled-braces"></a></p>

<h4 id="cuddled-braces">Cuddled braces</h4>

<p>Dropping whitespace between braces (aka “cuddling” them) for slice and array
literals is only permitted when both of the following are true.</p>

<ul>
  <li>The <a href="#literal-matching-braces">indentation matches</a></li>
  <li>The inner values are also literals or proto builders (i.e. not a variable or
other expression)</li>
</ul>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span> <span class="c">// Not cuddled</span>
        <span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"value"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"value"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{{</span> <span class="c">// Cuddled correctly</span>
    <span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"value"</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">},</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"value"</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">}}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">first</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="c">// Can't be cuddled</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"second"</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">okay</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">pb</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">pb</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Type_builder</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"first"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="c">// Proto Builders may be cuddled to save vertical space</span>
<span class="p">}</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Build</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">pb</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Type_builder</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"second"</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">}</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Build</span><span class="p">()}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">bad</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">first</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">Field</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"second"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">}}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="literal-repeated-type-names"></a></p>

<h4 id="repeated-type-names">Repeated type names</h4>

<p>Repeated type names may be omitted from slice and map literals. This can be
helpful in reducing clutter. A reasonable occasion for repeating the type names
explicitly is when dealing with a complex type that is not common in your
project, when the repetitive type names are on lines that are far apart and can
remind the reader of the context.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">42</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">43</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">repetitive</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="o">&amp;</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">42</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="o">&amp;</span><span class="n">Type</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">43</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">map</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">Type1</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type2</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">}</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">B</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">2</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">3</span><span class="p">}</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="n">B</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">4</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">repetitive</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">map</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">Type1</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Type2</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">Type1</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">}</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span><span class="n">Type2</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">B</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">2</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="n">Type1</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">A</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">3</span><span class="p">}</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span><span class="n">Type2</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">B</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">4</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><strong>Tip:</strong> If you want to remove repetitive type names in struct literals, you can
run <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">gofmt -s</code>.</p>

<p><a id="literal-zero-value-fields"></a></p>

<h4 id="zero-value-fields">Zero-value fields</h4>

<p><a href="https://golang.org/ref/spec#The_zero_value">Zero-value</a> fields may be omitted from struct literals when clarity is not lost
as a result.</p>

<p>Well-designed APIs often employ zero-value construction for enhanced
readability. For example, omitting the three zero-value fields from the
following struct draws attention to the only option that is being specified.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
  <span class="s">"github.com/golang/leveldb"</span>
  <span class="s">"github.com/golang/leveldb/db"</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">ldb</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">leveldb</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Open</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"/my/table"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Options</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">BlockSize</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="o">&lt;&lt;</span><span class="m">16</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">ErrorIfDBExists</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">true</span><span class="p">,</span>

    <span class="c">// These fields all have their zero values.</span>
    <span class="n">BlockRestartInterval</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">Comparer</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">nil</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">Compression</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">nil</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">FileSystem</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">nil</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">FilterPolicy</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">nil</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">MaxOpenFiles</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">WriteBufferSize</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">VerifyChecksums</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">})</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
  <span class="s">"github.com/golang/leveldb"</span>
  <span class="s">"github.com/golang/leveldb/db"</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">ldb</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">leveldb</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Open</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"/my/table"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Options</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">BlockSize</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="o">&lt;&lt;</span><span class="m">16</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">ErrorIfDBExists</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">true</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">})</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Structs within table-driven tests often benefit from <a href="#literal-field-names">explicit field names</a>,
especially when the test struct is not trivial. This allows the author to omit
the zero-valued fields entirely when the fields in question are not related to
the test case. For example, successful test cases should omit any error-related
or failure-related fields. In cases where the zero value is necessary to
understand the test case, such as testing for zero or <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code> inputs, the field
names should be specified.</p>

<p><strong>Concise</strong></p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="n">tests</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">input</span>      <span class="kt">string</span>
    <span class="n">wantPieces</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span>
    <span class="n">wantErr</span>    <span class="kt">error</span>
<span class="p">}{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">input</span><span class="o">:</span>      <span class="s">"1.2.3.4"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantPieces</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"1"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"2"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"3"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"4"</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">input</span><span class="o">:</span>   <span class="s">"hostname"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantErr</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="n">ErrBadHostname</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><strong>Explicit</strong></p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="n">tests</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">input</span>    <span class="kt">string</span>
    <span class="n">wantIPv4</span> <span class="kt">bool</span>
    <span class="n">wantIPv6</span> <span class="kt">bool</span>
    <span class="n">wantErr</span>  <span class="kt">bool</span>
<span class="p">}{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">input</span><span class="o">:</span>    <span class="s">"1.2.3.4"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantIPv4</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">true</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantIPv6</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">input</span><span class="o">:</span>    <span class="s">"1:2::3:4"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantIPv4</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantIPv6</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">true</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">input</span><span class="o">:</span>    <span class="s">"hostname"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantIPv4</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantIPv6</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="no">false</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="n">wantErr</span><span class="o">:</span>  <span class="no">true</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="nil-slices"></a></p>

<h3 id="nil-slices">Nil slices</h3>

<p>For most purposes, there is no functional difference between <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code> and the empty
slice. Built-in functions like <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">len</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cap</code> behave as expected on <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code>
slices.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">import</span> <span class="s">"fmt"</span>

<span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">s</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">int</span>         <span class="c">// nil</span>

<span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Println</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="p">)</span>      <span class="c">// []</span>
<span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Println</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="p">))</span> <span class="c">// 0</span>
<span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Println</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">cap</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="p">))</span> <span class="c">// 0</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="k">range</span> <span class="n">s</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="o">...</span><span class="p">}</span>   <span class="c">// no-op</span>

<span class="n">s</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">append</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">42</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Println</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="p">)</span>      <span class="c">// [42]</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>If you declare an empty slice as a local variable (especially if it can be the
source of a return value), prefer the nil initialization to reduce the risk of
bugs by callers.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">t</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">{}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Do not create APIs that force their clients to make distinctions between nil and
the empty slice.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// Ping pings its targets.</span>
<span class="c">// Returns hosts that successfully responded.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">Ping</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">hosts</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">([]</span><span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="kt">error</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span> <span class="o">...</span> <span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="c">// Ping pings its targets and returns a list of hosts</span>
<span class="c">// that successfully responded. Can be empty if the input was empty.</span>
<span class="c">// nil signifies that a system error occurred.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">Ping</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">hosts</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span> <span class="p">{</span> <span class="o">...</span> <span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>When designing interfaces, avoid making a distinction between a <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code> slice and
a non-<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code>, zero-length slice, as this can lead to subtle programming errors.
This is typically accomplished by using <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">len</code> to check for emptiness, rather
than <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">== nil</code>.</p>

<p>This implementation accepts both <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code> and zero-length slices as “empty”:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="c">// describeInts describes s with the given prefix, unless s is empty.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">describeInts</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">prefix</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">s</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">int</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="m">0</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="k">return</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
    <span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Println</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">prefix</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">s</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Instead of relying on the distinction as a part of the API:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">maybeInts</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">int</span> <span class="p">{</span> <span class="c">/* ... */</span> <span class="p">}</span>

<span class="c">// describeInts describes s with the given prefix; pass nil to skip completely.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">describeInts</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">prefix</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">s</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">int</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="c">// The behavior of this function unintentionally changes depending on what</span>
  <span class="c">// maybeInts() returns in 'empty' cases (nil or []int{}).</span>
  <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">s</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="k">return</span>
  <span class="p">}</span>
  <span class="n">fmt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Println</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">prefix</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">s</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>

<span class="n">describeInts</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Here are some ints:"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">maybeInts</span><span class="p">())</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>See <a href="#in-band-errors">in-band errors</a> for further discussion.</p>

<p><a id="indentation-confusion"></a></p>

<h3 id="indentation-confusion">Indentation confusion</h3>

<p>Avoid introducing a line break if it would align the rest of the line with an
indented code block. If this is unavoidable, leave a space to separate the code
in the block from the wrapped line.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">longCondition1</span> <span class="o">&amp;&amp;</span> <span class="n">longCondition2</span> <span class="o">&amp;&amp;</span>
    <span class="c">// Conditions 3 and 4 have the same indentation as the code within the if.</span>
    <span class="n">longCondition3</span> <span class="o">&amp;&amp;</span> <span class="n">longCondition4</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">log</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Info</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"all conditions met"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>See the following sections for specific guidelines and examples:</p>

<ul>
  <li><a href="#func-formatting">Function formatting</a></li>
  <li><a href="#conditional-formatting">Conditionals and loops</a></li>
  <li><a href="#literal-formatting">Literal formatting</a></li>
</ul>

<p><a id="func-formatting"></a></p>

<h3 id="function-formatting">Function formatting</h3>

<p>The signature of a function or method declaration should remain on a single line
to avoid <a href="#indentation-confusion">indentation confusion</a>.</p>

<p>Function argument lists can make some of the longest lines in a Go source file.
However, they precede a change in indentation, and therefore it is difficult to
break the line in a way that does not make subsequent lines look like part of
the function body in a confusing way:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">r</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">SomeType</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="n">SomeLongFunctionName</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">foo1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">foo2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">foo3</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">foo4</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">foo5</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">foo6</span> <span class="kt">int</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">foo7</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">bar</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">foo1</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="c">// ...</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>See <a href="best-practices#funcargs">best practices</a> for a few options for shortening
the call sites of functions that would otherwise have many arguments.</p>

<p>Lines can often be shortened by factoring out local variables.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">local</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">helper</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">some</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">parameters</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">here</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">foo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Call</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">list</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">of</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">parameters</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">local</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Similarly, function and method calls should not be separated based solely on
line length.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">foo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Call</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">long</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">list</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">of</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">parameters</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">all</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">on</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">one</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">line</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">bad</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">foo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Call</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">long</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">list</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">of</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">parameters</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">with</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">arbitrary</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">line</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">breaks</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Avoid adding inline comments to specific function arguments where possible.
Instead, use an <a href="best-practices#option-structure">option struct</a> or add more
detail to the function documentation.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">good</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">server</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">New</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">ctx</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">server</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Options</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="n">Port</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">42</span><span class="p">})</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">bad</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">server</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">New</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">ctx</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="m">42</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="c">// Port</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>If the API cannot be changed or if the local call is unusual (whether or not the
call is too long), it is always permissible to add line breaks if it aids in
understanding the call.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">canvas</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">RenderHeptagon</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">fillColor</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">x0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">vertexColor0</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">x1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">vertexColor1</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">x2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">vertexColor2</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">x3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">vertexColor3</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">x4</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y4</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">vertexColor4</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">x5</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y5</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">vertexColor5</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">x6</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y6</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">vertexColor6</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Note that the lines in the above example are not wrapped at a specific column
boundary but are grouped based on vertex coordinates and color.</p>

<p>Long string literals within functions should not be broken for the sake of line
length. For functions that include such strings, a line break can be added after
the string format, and the arguments can be provided on the next or subsequent
lines. The decision about where the line breaks should go is best made based on
semantic groupings of inputs, rather than based purely on line length.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">log</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Warningf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Database key (%q, %d, %q) incompatible in transaction started by (%q, %d, %q)"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">currentCustomer</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">currentOffset</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">currentKey</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">txCustomer</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">txOffset</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">txKey</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">log</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Warningf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Database key (%q, %d, %q) incompatible in"</span><span class="o">+</span>
    <span class="s">" transaction started by (%q, %d, %q)"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">currentCustomer</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">currentOffset</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">currentKey</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">txCustomer</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">txOffset</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">txKey</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="conditional-formatting"></a></p>

<h3 id="conditionals-and-loops">Conditionals and loops</h3>

<p>An <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">if</code> statement should not be line broken; multi-line <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">if</code> clauses can lead to
<a href="#indentation-confusion">indentation confusion</a>.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="c">// The second if statement is aligned with the code within the if block, causing</span>
<span class="c">// indentation confusion.</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">CurrentStatusIs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">InTransaction</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&amp;&amp;</span>
    <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ValuesEqual</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">TransactionKey</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">row</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Key</span><span class="p">())</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">TransactionError</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"query failed: row (%v): key does not match transaction key"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">row</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>If the short-circuit behavior is not required, the boolean operands can be
extracted directly:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">inTransaction</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">CurrentStatusIs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">InTransaction</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">keysMatch</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ValuesEqual</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">TransactionKey</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">row</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Key</span><span class="p">())</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">inTransaction</span> <span class="o">&amp;&amp;</span> <span class="n">keysMatch</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Error</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">TransactionError</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"query failed: row (%v): key does not match transaction key"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">row</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>There may also be other locals that can be extracted, especially if the
conditional is already repetitive:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">uid</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">user</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">GetUniqueUserID</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">UserIsAdmin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">uid</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">||</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">UserHasPermission</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">uid</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">perms</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ViewServerConfig</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">||</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">UserHasPermission</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">uid</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">perms</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">CreateGroup</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// ...</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">UserIsAdmin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">user</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">GetUniqueUserID</span><span class="p">())</span> <span class="o">||</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">UserHasPermission</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">user</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">GetUniqueUserID</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">perms</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ViewServerConfig</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">||</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">UserHasPermission</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">user</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">GetUniqueUserID</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">perms</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">CreateGroup</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// ...</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">if</code> statements that contain closures or multi-line struct literals should
ensure that the <a href="#literal-matching-braces">braces match</a> to avoid
<a href="#indentation-confusion">indentation confusion</a>.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">RunInTransaction</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="k">func</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">tx</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">db</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">TX</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="kt">error</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">tx</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Execute</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">userUpdate</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">z</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">});</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
