<h3 id="identify-the-function">Identify the function</h3>

<p>In most tests, failure messages should include the name of the function that
failed, even though it seems obvious from the name of the test function.
Specifically, your failure message should be <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">YourFunc(%v) = %v, want %v</code>
instead of just <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">got %v, want %v</code>.</p>

<p><a id="identify-the-input"></a></p>

<h3 id="identify-the-input">Identify the input</h3>

<p>In most tests, failure messages should include the function inputs if they are
short. If the relevant properties of the inputs are not obvious (for example,
because the inputs are large or opaque), you should name your test cases with a
description of what’s being tested and print the description as part of your
error message.</p>

<p><a id="got-before-want"></a></p>

<h3 id="got-before-want">Got before want</h3>

<p>Test outputs should include the actual value that the function returned before
printing the value that was expected. A standard format for printing test
outputs is <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">YourFunc(%v) = %v, want %v</code>. Where you would write “actual” and
“expected”, prefer using the words “got” and “want”, respectively.</p>

<p>For diffs, directionality is less apparent, and as such it is important to
include a key to aid in interpreting the failure. See the
<a href="#print-diffs">section on printing diffs</a>. Whichever diff order you use in your failure
messages, you should explicitly indicate it as a part of the failure message,
because existing code is inconsistent about the ordering.</p>

<p><a id="compare-full-structures"></a></p>

<h3 id="full-structure-comparisons">Full structure comparisons</h3>

<p>If your function returns a struct (or any data type with multiple fields such as
slices, arrays, and maps), avoid writing test code that performs a hand-coded
field-by-field comparison of the struct. Instead, construct the data that you’re
expecting your function to return, and compare directly using a
<a href="#types-of-equality">deep comparison</a>.</p>

<p><strong>Note:</strong> This does not apply if your data contains irrelevant fields that
obscure the intention of the test.</p>

<p>If your struct needs to be compared for approximate (or equivalent kind of
semantic) equality or it contains fields that cannot be compared for equality
(e.g., if one of the fields is an <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">io.Reader</code>), tweaking a <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp#Diff"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp.Diff</code></a> or
<a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp#Equal"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp.Equal</code></a> comparison with <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp/cmpopts"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmpopts</code></a> options such as
<a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp/cmpopts#IgnoreInterfaces"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmpopts.IgnoreInterfaces</code></a> may meet your needs
(<a href="https://play.golang.org/p/vrCUNVfxsvF">example</a>).</p>

<p>If your function returns multiple return values, you don’t need to wrap those in
a struct before comparing them. Just compare the return values individually and
print them.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">val</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">multi</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">tail</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">strconv</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">UnquoteChar</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">`\"Fran &amp; Freddie's Diner\"`</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="sc">'"'</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Fatalf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="o">...</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">val</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="s">`"`</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="o">...</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">multi</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="o">...</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">tail</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="s">`Fran &amp; Freddie's Diner"`</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="o">...</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="compare-stable-results"></a></p>

<h3 id="compare-stable-results">Compare stable results</h3>

<p>Avoid comparing results that may depend on output stability of a package that
you do not own. Instead, the test should compare on semantically relevant
information that is stable and resistant to changes in dependencies. For
functionality that returns a formatted string or serialized bytes, it is
generally not safe to assume that the output is stable.</p>

<p>For example, <a href="https://golang.org/pkg/encoding/json/#Marshal"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">json.Marshal</code></a> can change (and has changed in the past) the
specific bytes that it emits. Tests that perform string equality on the JSON
string may break if the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">json</code> package changes how it serializes the bytes.
Instead, a more robust test would parse the contents of the JSON string and
ensure that it is semantically equivalent to some expected data structure.</p>

<p><a id="keep-going"></a></p>

<h3 id="keep-going">Keep going</h3>

<p>Tests should keep going for as long as possible, even after a failure, in order
to print out all of the failed checks in a single run. This way, a developer who
is fixing the failing test doesn’t have to re-run the test after fixing each bug
to find the next bug.</p>

<p>Prefer calling <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Error</code> over <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Fatal</code> for reporting a mismatch. When comparing
several different properties of a function’s output, use <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Error</code> for each of
those comparisons.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">gotMean</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">gotVariance</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">MyDistribution</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Fatalf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"MyDistribution(%v) returned unexpected error: %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">diff</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">cmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Diff</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">wantMean</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">gotMean</span><span class="p">);</span> <span class="n">diff</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="s">""</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"MyDistribution(%v) returned unexpected difference in mean value (-want +got):</span><span class="se">\n</span><span class="s">%s"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">diff</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">diff</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">cmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Diff</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">wantVariance</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">gotVariance</span><span class="p">);</span> <span class="n">diff</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="s">""</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"MyDistribution(%v) returned unexpected difference in variance value (-want +got):</span><span class="se">\n</span><span class="s">%s"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">diff</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Calling <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Fatal</code> is primarily useful for reporting an unexpected condition
(such as an error or output mismatch) when subsequent failures would be
meaningless or even mislead the investigator. Note how the code below calls
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Fatalf</code> and <em>then</em> <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Errorf</code>:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">gotEncoded</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Encode</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">gotEncoded</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">wantEncoded</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Fatalf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Encode(%q) = %q, want %q"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">gotEncoded</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">wantEncoded</span><span class="p">)</span>
  <span class="c">// It doesn't make sense to decode from unexpected encoded input.</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="n">gotDecoded</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Decode</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">gotEncoded</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Fatalf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) returned unexpected error: %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">gotEncoded</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">gotDecoded</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">input</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) = %q, want %q"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">gotEncoded</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">gotDecoded</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">input</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>For table-driven test, consider using subtests and use <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Fatal</code> rather than
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Error</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">continue</code>. See also
<a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/index.html#gotip">GoTip #25: Subtests: Making Your Tests Lean</a>.</p>

<p><strong>Best practice:</strong> For more discussion about when <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Fatal</code> should be used, see
<a href="best-practices#t-fatal">best practices</a>.</p>

<p><a id="types-of-equality"></a></p>

<h3 id="equality-comparison-and-diffs">Equality comparison and diffs</h3>

<p>The <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">==</code> operator evaluates equality using <a href="http://golang.org/ref/spec#Comparison_operators">language-defined comparisons</a>.
Scalar values (numbers, booleans, etc) are compared based on their values, but
only some structs and interfaces can be compared in this way. Pointers are
compared based on whether they point to the same variable, rather than based on
the equality of the values to which they point.</p>

<p>The <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code></a> package can compare more complex data structures not appropriately
handled by <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">==</code>, such as slices. Use <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp#Equal"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp.Equal</code></a> for equality comparison and
<a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp#Diff"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp.Diff</code></a> to obtain a human-readable diff between objects.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">want</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span><span class="n">Doc</span><span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">Type</span><span class="o">:</span>     <span class="s">"blogPost"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">Comments</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">2</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">Body</span><span class="o">:</span>     <span class="s">"This is the post body."</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">Authors</span><span class="o">:</span>  <span class="p">[]</span><span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"isaac"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"albert"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"emmy"</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="o">!</span><span class="n">cmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Equal</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"AddPost() = %+v, want %+v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>As a general-purpose comparison library, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> may not know how to compare
certain types. For example, it can only compare protocol buffer messages if
passed the <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/google.golang.org/protobuf/testing/protocmp#Transform"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">protocmp.Transform</code></a> option.</p>

<!-- The order of want and got here is deliberate. See comment in #print-diffs. -->

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">diff</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">cmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Diff</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">want</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">protocmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Transform</span><span class="p">());</span> <span class="n">diff</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="s">""</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Foo() returned unexpected difference in protobuf messages (-want +got):</span><span class="se">\n</span><span class="s">%s"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">diff</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Although the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> package is not part of the Go standard library, it is
maintained by the Go team and should produce stable equality results over time.
It is user-configurable and should serve most comparison needs.</p>

<p>Existing code may make use of the following older libraries, and may continue
using them for consistency:</p>

<ul>
  <li><a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/kylelemons/godebug/pretty"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">pretty</code></a> produces aesthetically pleasing difference reports. However, it
quite deliberately considers values that have the same visual representation
as equal. In particular, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">pretty</code> does not catch differences between nil
slices and empty ones, is not sensitive to different interface
implementations with identical fields, and it is possible to use a nested
map as the basis for comparison with a struct value. It also serializes the
entire value into a string before producing a diff, and as such is not a
good choice for comparing large values. By default, it compares unexported
fields, which makes it sensitive to changes in implementation details in
your dependencies. For this reason, it is not appropriate to use <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">pretty</code> on
protobuf messages.</li>
</ul>

<p>Prefer using <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> for new code, and it is worth considering updating older code
to use <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> where and when it is practical to do so.</p>

<p>Older code may use the standard library <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">reflect.DeepEqual</code> function to compare
complex structures. <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">reflect.DeepEqual</code> should not be used for checking
equality, as it is sensitive to changes in unexported fields and other
implementation details. Code that is using <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">reflect.DeepEqual</code> should be updated
to one of the above libraries.</p>

<p><strong>Note:</strong> The <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> package is designed for testing, rather than production use.
As such, it may panic when it suspects that a comparison is performed
incorrectly to provide instruction to users on how to improve the test to be
less brittle. Given cmp’s propensity towards panicking, it makes it unsuitable
for code that is used in production as a spurious panic may be fatal.</p>

<p><a id="level-of-detail"></a></p>

<h3 id="level-of-detail">Level of detail</h3>

<p>The conventional failure message, which is suitable for most Go tests, is
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">YourFunc(%v) = %v, want %v</code>. However, there are cases that may call for more or
less detail:</p>

<ul>
  <li>Tests performing complex interactions should describe the interactions too.
For example, if the same <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">YourFunc</code> is called several times, identify which
call failed the test. If it’s important to know any extra state of the
system, include that in the failure output (or at least in the logs).</li>
  <li>If the data is a complex struct with significant boilerplate, it is
acceptable to describe only the important parts in the message, but do not
overly obscure the data.</li>
  <li>Setup failures do not require the same level of detail. If a test helper
populates a Spanner table but Spanner was down, you probably don’t need to
include which test input you were going to store in the database.
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Fatalf("Setup: Failed to set up test database: %s", err)</code> is usually
helpful enough to resolve the issue.</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>Tip:</strong> Make your failure mode trigger during development. Review what the
failure message looks like and whether a maintainer can effectively deal with
the failure.</p>

<p>There are some techniques for reproducing test inputs and outputs clearly:</p>

<ul>
  <li>When printing string data, <a href="#use-percent-q"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">%q</code> is often useful</a> to
emphasize that the value is important and to more easily spot bad values.</li>
  <li>When printing (small) structs, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">%+v</code> can be more useful than <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">%v</code>.</li>
  <li>When validation of larger values fails, <a href="#print-diffs">printing a diff</a> can
make it easier to understand the failure.</li>
</ul>

<p><a id="print-diffs"></a></p>

<h3 id="print-diffs">Print diffs</h3>

<p>If your function returns large output then it can be hard for someone reading
the failure message to find the differences when your test fails. Instead of
printing both the returned value and the wanted value, make a diff.</p>

<p>To compute diffs for such values, <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp.Diff</code> is preferred, particularly for new
tests and new code, but other tools may be used. See <a href="#types-of-equality">types of equality</a> for
guidance regarding the strengths and weaknesses of each function.</p>

<ul>
  <li>
    <p><a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp#Diff"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp.Diff</code></a></p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/kylelemons/godebug/pretty#Compare"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">pretty.Compare</code></a></p>
  </li>
</ul>

<p>You can use the <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/kylelemons/godebug/diff"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">diff</code></a> package to compare multi-line strings or lists of
strings. You can use this as a building block for other kinds of diffs.</p>

<p>Add some text to your failure message explaining the direction of the diff.</p>

<!--
The reversed order of want and got in these examples is intentional, as this is
the prevailing order across the Google codebase. The lack of a stance on which
order to use is also intentional, as there is no consensus which is
"most readable."


-->

<ul>
  <li>
    <p>Something like <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">diff (-want +got)</code> is good when you’re using the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code>,
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">pretty</code>, and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">diff</code> packages (if you pass <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">(want, got)</code> to the function),
because the <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">-</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">+</code> that you add to your format string will match the
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">-</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">+</code> that actually appear at the beginning of the diff lines. If you
pass <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">(got, want)</code> to your function, the correct key would be <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">(-got +want)</code>
instead.</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>The <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">messagediff</code> package uses a different output format, so the message
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">diff (want -&gt; got)</code> is appropriate when you’re using it (if you pass
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">(want, got)</code> to the function), because the direction of the arrow will
match the direction of the arrow in the “modified” lines.</p>
  </li>
</ul>

<p>The diff will span multiple lines, so you should print a newline before you
print the diff.</p>

<p><a id="test-error-semantics"></a></p>

<h3 id="test-error-semantics">Test error semantics</h3>

<p>When a unit test performs string comparisons or uses a vanilla <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> to check
that particular kinds of errors are returned for particular inputs, you may find
that your tests are brittle if any of those error messages are reworded in the
future. Since this has the potential to turn your unit test into a change
detector (see <a href="https://testing.googleblog.com/2015/01/testing-on-toilet-change-detector-tests.html">TotT: Change-Detector Tests Considered Harmful</a> ),
don’t use string comparison to check what type of error your function returns.
However, it is permissible to use string comparisons to check that error
messages coming from the package under test satisfy certain properties, for
example, that it includes the parameter name.</p>

<p>Error values in Go typically have a component intended for human eyes and a
component intended for semantic control flow. Tests should seek to only test
semantic information that can be reliably observed, rather than display
information that is intended for human debugging, as this is often subject to
future changes. For guidance on constructing errors with semantic meaning see
<a href="best-practices#error-handling">best-practices regarding errors</a>. If an error
with insufficient semantic information is coming from a dependency outside your
control, consider filing a bug against the owner to help improve the API, rather
than relying on parsing the error message.</p>

<p>Within unit tests, it is common to only care whether an error occurred or not.
If so, then it is sufficient to only test whether the error was non-nil when you
expected an error. If you would like to test that the error semantically matches
some other error, then consider using <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/errors#Is"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">errors.Is</code></a> or <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> with
<a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp/cmpopts#EquateErrors"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmpopts.EquateErrors</code></a>.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p><strong>Note:</strong> If a test uses <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp/cmpopts#EquateErrors"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmpopts.EquateErrors</code></a> but all of its <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">wantErr</code>
values are either <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">nil</code> or <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmpopts.AnyError</code>, then using <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">cmp</code> is
<a href="guide#least-mechanism">unnecessary mechanism</a>. Simplify the code by making
the want field a <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">bool</code>. You can then use a simple comparison with <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">!=</code>.</p>

  <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">f</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">gotErr</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span><span class="p">;</span> <span class="n">gotErr</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">wantErr</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"f(%q) = %v, want error presence = %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">wantErr</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div>  </div>
</blockquote>

<p>See also
<a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/index.html#gotip">GoTip #13: Designing Errors for Checking</a>.</p>

<p><a id="test-structure"></a></p>

<h2 id="test-structure">Test structure</h2>

<p><a id="subtests"></a></p>

<h3 id="subtests">Subtests</h3>

<p>The standard Go testing library offers a facility to <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/testing#hdr-Subtests_and_Sub_benchmarks">define subtests</a>. This
allows flexibility in setup and cleanup, controlling parallelism, and test
filtering. Subtests can be useful (particularly for table-driven tests), but
using them is not mandatory. See also the
<a href="https://blog.golang.org/subtests">Go blog post about subtests</a>.</p>

<p>Subtests should not depend on the execution of other cases for success or
initial state, because subtests are expected to be able to be run individually
with using <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">go test -run</code> flags or with Bazel <a href="https://bazel.build/docs/user-manual#test-filter">test filter</a> expressions.</p>

<p><a id="subtest-names"></a></p>

<h4 id="subtest-names">Subtest names</h4>

<p>Name your subtest such that it is readable in test output and useful on the
command line for users of test filtering. When you use <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Run</code> to create a
subtest, the first argument is used as a descriptive name for the test. To
ensure that test results are legible to humans reading the logs, choose subtest
names that will remain useful and readable after escaping. Think of subtest
names more like a function identifier than a prose description.</p>

<p>The test runner replaces spaces with underscores, and escapes non-printing
characters. To ensure accurate correlation between test logs and source code, it
is recommended to avoid using these characters in subtest names.</p>

<p>If your test data benefits from a longer description, consider putting the
description in a separate field (perhaps to be printed using <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Log</code> or
alongside failure messages).</p>

<p>Subtests may be run individually using flags to the <a href="https://golang.org/cmd/go/#hdr-Testing_flags">Go test runner</a> or Bazel
<a href="https://bazel.build/docs/user-manual#test-filter">test filter</a>, so choose descriptive names that are also easy to type.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p><strong>Warning:</strong> Slash characters are particularly unfriendly in subtest names,
since they have <a href="https://blog.golang.org/subtests#:~:text=Perhaps%20a%20bit,match%20any%20tests">special meaning for test filters</a>.</p>

  <blockquote>
    <div class="language-sh highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c"># Bad:</span>
<span class="c"># Assuming TestTime and t.Run("America/New_York", ...)</span>
bazel <span class="nb">test</span> :mytest <span class="nt">--test_filter</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"Time/New_York"</span>    <span class="c"># Runs nothing!</span>
bazel <span class="nb">test</span> :mytest <span class="nt">--test_filter</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"Time//New_York"</span>   <span class="c"># Correct, but awkward.</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>
  </blockquote>
</blockquote>

<p>To <a href="#identify-the-input">identify the inputs</a> of the function, include them in the test’s failure
messages, where they won’t be escaped by the test runner.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">TestTranslate</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">data</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">desc</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">srcLang</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">dstLang</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">srcText</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">wantDstText</span> <span class="kt">string</span>
    <span class="p">}{</span>
        <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">name</span><span class="o">:</span>        <span class="s">"hu=en_bug-1234"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">desc</span><span class="o">:</span>        <span class="s">"regression test following bug 1234. contact: cleese"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">srcLang</span><span class="o">:</span>     <span class="s">"hu"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">srcText</span><span class="o">:</span>     <span class="s">"cigarettát és egy öngyújtót kérek"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">dstLang</span><span class="o">:</span>     <span class="s">"en"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">wantDstText</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="s">"cigarettes and a lighter please"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="p">},</span> <span class="c">// ...</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">_</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">range</span> <span class="n">data</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Run</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="k">func</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Translate</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">srcLang</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">dstLang</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">srcText</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">wantDstText</span> <span class="p">{</span>
                <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"%s</span><span class="se">\n</span><span class="s">Translate(%q, %q, %q) = %q, want %q"</span><span class="p">,</span>
                    <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">desc</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">srcLang</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">dstLang</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">srcText</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">wantDstText</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="p">}</span>
        <span class="p">})</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Here are a few examples of things to avoid:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="c">// Too wordy.</span>
<span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Run</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"check that there is no mention of scratched records or hovercrafts"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">...</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="c">// Slashes cause problems on the command line.</span>
<span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Run</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"AM/PM confusion"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">...</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>See also
<a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/index.html#gotip">Go Tip #117: Subtest Names</a>.</p>

<p><a id="table-driven-tests"></a></p>

<h3 id="table-driven-tests">Table-driven tests</h3>

<p>Use table-driven tests when many different test cases can be tested using
similar testing logic.</p>

<ul>
  <li>When testing whether the actual output of a function is equal to the
expected output. For example, the many <a href="https://cs.opensource.google/go/go/+/master:src/fmt/fmt_test.go">tests of <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">fmt.Sprintf</code></a> or the
minimal snippet below.</li>
  <li>When testing whether the outputs of a function always conform to the same
set of invariants. For example, <a href="https://cs.opensource.google/go/go/+/master:src/net/dial_test.go;l=318;drc=5b606a9d2b7649532fe25794fa6b99bd24e7697c">tests for <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">net.Dial</code></a>.</li>
</ul>

<p>Here is the minimal structure of a table-driven test. If needed, you may use
different names or add extra facilities such as subtests or setup and cleanup
functions. Always keep <a href="#useful-test-failures">useful test failures</a> in mind.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">TestCompare</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">compareTests</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">a</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">b</span> <span class="kt">string</span>
        <span class="n">want</span> <span class="kt">int</span>
    <span class="p">}{</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">""</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">""</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"a"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">""</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">""</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"a"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">-</span><span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"abc"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"abc"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"ab"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"abc"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">-</span><span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"abc"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"ab"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"x"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"ab"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"ab"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"x"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">-</span><span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"x"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"a"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"b"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"x"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">-</span><span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="c">// test runtime·memeq's chunked implementation</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"abcdefgh"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"abcdefgh"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"abcdefghi"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"abcdefghi"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"abcdefghi"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"abcdefghj"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="o">-</span><span class="m">1</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>

    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">_</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">range</span> <span class="n">compareTests</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Compare</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">a</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">b</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">want</span> <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Compare(%q, %q) = %v, want %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">a</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">b</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="p">}</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><strong>Note</strong>: The failure messages in this example above fulfill the guidance to
<a href="#identify-the-function">identify the function</a> and
<a href="#identify-the-input">identify the input</a>. There’s no need to
<a href="#table-tests-identifying-the-row">identify the row numerically</a>.</p>

<p>When some test cases need to be checked using different logic from other test
cases, it is appropriate to write multiple test functions, as explained in
<a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/index.html#gotip">GoTip #50: Disjoint Table Tests</a>.</p>

<p>When the additional test cases are simple (e.g., basic error checking) and don’t
introduce conditionalized code flow in the table test’s loop body, it’s
permissible to include that case in the existing test, though be careful using
logic like this. What starts simple today can organically grow into something
unmaintainable.</p>

<p>For example:</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">TestDivide</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">dividend</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">divisor</span> <span class="kt">int</span>
        <span class="n">want</span>              <span class="kt">int</span>
        <span class="n">wantErr</span>           <span class="kt">bool</span>
    <span class="p">}{</span>
        <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">dividend</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">4</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">divisor</span><span class="o">:</span>  <span class="m">2</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">want</span><span class="o">:</span>     <span class="m">2</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">dividend</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">10</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">divisor</span><span class="o">:</span>  <span class="m">2</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">want</span><span class="o">:</span>     <span class="m">5</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="p">},</span>
        <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">dividend</span><span class="o">:</span> <span class="m">1</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">divisor</span><span class="o">:</span>  <span class="m">0</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">wantErr</span><span class="o">:</span>  <span class="no">true</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>

    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">_</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">range</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Divide</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">dividend</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">divisor</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">wantErr</span> <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Divide(%d, %d) error = %v, want error presence = %t"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">dividend</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">divisor</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">wantErr</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="p">}</span>

        <span class="c">// In this example, we're only testing the value result when the tested function didn't fail.</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="k">continue</span>
        <span class="p">}</span>

        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">want</span> <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Divide(%d, %d) = %d, want %d"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">dividend</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">divisor</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="p">}</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>More complicated logic in your test code, like complex error checking based on
conditional differences in test setup (often based on table test input
parameters), can be <a href="guide#maintainability">difficult to understand</a> when each
entry in a table has specialized logic based on the inputs. If test cases have
different logic but identical setup, a sequence of <a href="#subtests">subtests</a> within
a single test function might be more readable. A test helper may also be useful
for simplifying test setup in order to maintain the readability of a test body.</p>

<p>You can combine table-driven tests with multiple test functions. For example,
when testing that a function’s output exactly matches the expected output and
that the function returns a non-nil error for an invalid input, then writing two
separate table-driven test functions is the best approach: one for normal
non-error outputs, and one for error outputs.</p>

<p><a id="table-tests-data-driven"></a></p>

<h4 id="data-driven-test-cases">Data-driven test cases</h4>

<p>Table test rows can sometimes become complicated, with the row values dictating
conditional behavior inside the test case. The extra clarity from the
duplication between the test cases is necessary for readability.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">type</span> <span class="n">decodeCase</span> <span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">name</span>   <span class="kt">string</span>
    <span class="n">input</span>  <span class="kt">string</span>
    <span class="n">output</span> <span class="kt">string</span>
    <span class="n">err</span>    <span class="kt">error</span>
<span class="p">}</span>

<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">TestDecode</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// setupCodex is slow as it creates a real Codex for the test.</span>
    <span class="n">codex</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">setupCodex</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span><span class="p">)</span>

    <span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="n">decodeCase</span> <span class="c">// rows omitted for brevity</span>

    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">_</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">range</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Run</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="k">func</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">output</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Decode</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">codex</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">output</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">output</span><span class="p">;</span> <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="p">{</span>
                <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) = %v, want %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="p">}</span>
            <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">err</span><span class="p">;</span> <span class="o">!</span><span class="n">cmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Equal</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
                <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) err %q, want %q"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="p">}</span>
        <span class="p">})</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
<span class="p">}</span>

<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">TestDecodeWithFake</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="c">// A fakeCodex is a fast approximation of a real Codex.</span>
    <span class="n">codex</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">newFakeCodex</span><span class="p">()</span>

    <span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="n">decodeCase</span> <span class="c">// rows omitted for brevity</span>

    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">_</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">range</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Run</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="k">func</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
            <span class="n">output</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Decode</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">codex</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">output</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">output</span><span class="p">;</span> <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="p">{</span>
                <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) = %v, want %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="p">}</span>
            <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">err</span><span class="p">;</span> <span class="o">!</span><span class="n">cmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Equal</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
                <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) err %q, want %q"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="p">}</span>
        <span class="p">})</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>In the counterexample below, note how hard it is to distinguish between which
type of <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">Codex</code> is used per test case in the case setup. (The highlighted parts
run afoul of the advice from <a href="https://testing.googleblog.com/2008/09/tott-data-driven-traps.html">TotT: Data Driven Traps!</a> .)</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">type</span> <span class="n">decodeCase</span> <span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="n">name</span>   <span class="kt">string</span>
  <span class="n">input</span>  <span class="kt">string</span>
  <span class="n">codex</span>  <span class="n">testCodex</span>
  <span class="n">output</span> <span class="kt">string</span>
  <span class="n">err</span>    <span class="kt">error</span>
<span class="p">}</span>

<span class="k">type</span> <span class="n">testCodex</span> <span class="kt">int</span>

<span class="k">const</span> <span class="p">(</span>
  <span class="n">fake</span> <span class="n">testCodex</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="no">iota</span>
  <span class="n">prod</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">TestDecode</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
  <span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="n">decodeCase</span> <span class="c">// rows omitted for brevity</span>

  <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">_</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Run</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="k">func</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
      <span class="k">var</span> <span class="n">codex</span> <span class="n">Codex</span>
      <span class="k">switch</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">codex</span> <span class="p">{</span>
      <span class="k">case</span> <span class="n">fake</span><span class="o">:</span>
        <span class="n">codex</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">newFakeCodex</span><span class="p">()</span>
      <span class="k">case</span> <span class="n">prod</span><span class="o">:</span>
        <span class="n">codex</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">setupCodex</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span><span class="p">)</span>
      <span class="k">default</span><span class="o">:</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Fatalf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Unknown codex type: %v"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">codex</span><span class="p">)</span>
      <span class="p">}</span>
      <span class="n">output</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">Decode</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">codex</span><span class="p">)</span>
      <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">output</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">output</span><span class="p">;</span> <span class="n">got</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) = %q, want %q"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
      <span class="p">}</span>
      <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">err</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">err</span><span class="p">;</span> <span class="o">!</span><span class="n">cmp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Equal</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Decode(%q) err %q, want %q"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">test</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">got</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span><span class="p">)</span>
      <span class="p">}</span>
    <span class="p">})</span>
  <span class="p">}</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p><a id="table-tests-identifying-the-row"></a></p>

<h4 id="identifying-the-row">Identifying the row</h4>

<p>Do not use the index of the test in the test table as a substitute for naming
your tests or printing the inputs. Nobody wants to go through your test table
and count the entries in order to figure out which test case is failing.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="n">tests</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="p">[]</span><span class="k">struct</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">input</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">want</span> <span class="kt">string</span>
<span class="p">}{</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"hello"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"HELLO"</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="p">{</span><span class="s">"wORld"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"WORLD"</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">i</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">d</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="k">range</span> <span class="n">tests</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">strings</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ToUpper</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">input</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="n">d</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">want</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Errorf</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s">"Failed on case #%d"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">i</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Add a test description to your test struct and print it along failure messages.
When using subtests, your subtest name should be effective in identifying the
row.</p>

<p><strong>Important:</strong> Even though <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Run</code> scopes the output and execution, you must
always <a href="#identify-the-input">identify the input</a>. The table test row names must follow the
<a href="#subtest-names">subtest naming</a> guidance.</p>

<p><a id="mark-test-helpers"></a></p>

<h3 id="test-helpers">Test helpers</h3>

<p>A test helper is a function that performs a setup or cleanup task. All failures
that occur in test helpers are expected to be failures of the environment (not
from the code under test) — for example when a test database cannot be started
because there are no more free ports on this machine.</p>

<p>If you pass a <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">*testing.T</code>, call <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/testing#T.Helper"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Helper</code></a> to attribute failures in the test
helper to the line where the helper is called. This parameter should come after
a <a href="#contexts">context</a> parameter, if present, and before any remaining
parameters.</p>

<div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">TestSomeFunction</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">golden</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">readFile</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s">"testdata/golden-result.txt"</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="c">// ... tests against golden ...</span>
<span class="p">}</span>

<span class="c">// readFile returns the contents of a data file.</span>
<span class="c">// It must only be called from the same goroutine as started the test.</span>
<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">readFile</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">*</span><span class="n">testing</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">filename</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="kt">string</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Helper</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">contents</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">:=</span> <span class="n">runfiles</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ReadFile</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">filename</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">err</span> <span class="o">!=</span> <span class="no">nil</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Fatal</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">err</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="p">}</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">contents</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">}</span>
</code></pre></div></div>

<p>Do not use this pattern when it obscures the connection between a test failure
and the conditions that led to it. Specifically, the guidance about
<a href="#assert">assert libraries</a> still applies, and <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/testing#T.Helper"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">t.Helper</code></a> should not be used
to implement such libraries.</p>

<p><strong>Tip:</strong> For more on the distinction between test helpers and assertion helpers,
see <a href="best-practices#test-functions">best practices</a>.</p>

<p>Although the above refers to <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">*testing.T</code>, much of the advice stays the same for
benchmark and fuzz helpers.</p>

<p><a id="test-package"></a></p>

<h3 id="test-package">Test package</h3>

<p><a id="TOC-TestPackage"></a></p>

<p><a id="test-same-package"></a></p>

<h4 id="tests-in-the-same-package">Tests in the same package</h4>

<p>Tests may be defined in the same package as the code being tested.</p>

<p>To write a test in the same package:</p>

<ul>
  <li>Place the tests in a <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">foo_test.go</code> file</li>
  <li>Use <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">package foo</code> for the test file</li>
  <li>Do not explicitly import the package to be tested</li>
</ul>

<pre><code class="language-build"># Good:
go_library(
    name = "foo",
    srcs = ["foo.go"],
    deps = [
        ...
    ],
)

go_test(
    name = "foo_test",
    size = "small",
    srcs = ["foo_test.go"],
    library = ":foo",
    deps = [
        ...
    ],
)
</code></pre>

<p>A test in the same package can access unexported identifiers in the package.
This may enable better test coverage and more concise tests. Be aware that any
<a href="#examples">examples</a> declared in the test will not have the package names that a user will
need in their code.</p>

<p><a id="test-different-package"></a></p>

<h4 id="tests-in-a-different-package">Tests in a different package</h4>

<p>It is not always appropriate or even possible to define a test in the same
package as the code being tested. In these cases, use a package name with the
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">_test</code> suffix. This is an exception to the “no underscores” rule to
<a href="#package-names">package names</a>. For example:</p>

<ul>
  <li>
    <p>If an integration test does not have an obvious library that it belongs to</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">gmailintegration_test</span>

<span class="k">import</span> <span class="s">"testing"</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>If defining the tests in the same package results in circular dependencies</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">fireworks_test</span>

<span class="k">import</span> <span class="p">(</span>
  <span class="s">"fireworks"</span>
  <span class="s">"fireworkstestutil"</span> <span class="c">// fireworkstestutil also imports fireworks</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>
  </li>
</ul>

<p><a id="use-package-testing"></a></p>

<h3 id="use-package-testing">Use package <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">testing</code></h3>

<p>The Go standard library provides the <a href="https://pkg.go.dev/testing"><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">testing</code> package</a>. This is the only
testing framework permitted for Go code in the Google codebase. In particular,
<a href="#assert">assertion libraries</a> and third-party testing frameworks are not
allowed.</p>

<p>The <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">testing</code> package provides a minimal but complete set of functionality for
writing good tests:</p>

<ul>
  <li>Top-level tests</li>
  <li>Benchmarks</li>
  <li><a href="https://blog.golang.org/examples">Runnable examples</a></li>
  <li>Subtests</li>
  <li>Logging</li>
  <li>Failures and fatal failures</li>
</ul>

<p>These are designed to work cohesively with core language features like
<a href="https://go.dev/ref/spec#Composite_literals">composite literal</a> and <a href="https://go.dev/ref/spec#If_statements">if-with-initializer</a> syntax to enable test authors to
write [clear, readable, and maintainable tests].</p>

<p><a id="non-decisions"></a></p>

<h2 id="non-decisions">Non-decisions</h2>

<p>A style guide cannot enumerate positive prescriptions for all matters, nor can
it enumerate all matters about which it does not offer an opinion. That said,
here are a few things where the readability community has previously debated and
has not achieved consensus about.</p>

<ul>
  <li><strong>Local variable initialization with zero value</strong>. <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">var i int</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">i := 0</code>
are equivalent. See also <a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/best-practices#vardeclinitialization">initialization best practices</a>.</li>
  <li><strong>Empty composite literal vs. <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">new</code> or <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">make</code></strong>. <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">&amp;File{}</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">new(File)</code>
are equivalent. So are <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">map[string]bool{}</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">make(map[string]bool)</code>. See
also <a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/best-practices#vardeclcomposite">composite declaration best practices</a>.</li>
  <li><strong>got, want argument ordering in cmp.Diff calls</strong>. Be locally consistent,
and <a href="#print-diffs">include a legend</a> in your failure message.</li>
  <li><strong><code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">errors.New</code> vs <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">fmt.Errorf</code> on non-formatted strings</strong>.
<code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">errors.New("foo")</code> and <code class="language-plaintext highlighter-rouge">fmt.Errorf("foo")</code> may be used interchangeably.</li>
</ul>

<p>If there are special circumstances where they come up again, the readability
mentor might make an optional comment, but in general the author is free to pick
the style they prefer in the given situation.</p>

<p>Naturally, if anything not covered by the style guide does need more discussion,
authors are welcome to ask – either in the specific review, or on internal
message boards.</p>

      <h1><a href="https://google.github.io/styleguide/">styleguide</a></h1>
      

      <!--* toc_depth: 3 *-->

<h1 id="go-style-best-practices">Go Style Best Practices</h1>

<p>https://google.github.io/styleguide/go/best-practices</p>

<p><a href="index">Overview</a> | <a href="guide">Guide</a> | <a href="decisions">Decisions</a> |
<a href="best-practices">Best practices</a></p>

<!--

-->

<p><strong>Note:</strong> This is part of a series of documents that outline <a href="index">Go Style</a>
at Google. This document is <strong>neither <a href="index#normative">normative</a> nor
<a href="index#canonical">canonical</a></strong>, and is an auxiliary document to the
<a href="guide">core style guide</a>. See <a href="index#about">the overview</a> for more information.</p>

<p><a id="about"></a></p>

<h2 id="about">About</h2>

<p>This file documents <strong>guidance about how to best apply the Go Style Guide</strong>.
This guidance is intended for common situations that arise frequently, but may
not apply in every circumstance. Where possible, multiple alternative approaches
are discussed along with the considerations that go into the decision about when
and when not to apply them.</p>

<p>See <a href="index#about">the overview</a> for the full set of Style Guide documents.</p>

<p><a id="naming"></a></p>

<h2 id="naming">Naming</h2>

<p><a id="function-names"></a></p>

<h3 id="function-and-method-names">Function and method names</h3>

<p><a id="function-name-repetition"></a></p>

<h4 id="avoid-repetition">Avoid repetition</h4>

<p>When choosing the name for a function or method, consider the context in which
the name will be read. Consider the following recommendations to avoid excess
<a href="decisions#repetition">repetition</a> at the call site:</p>

<ul>
  <li>
    <p>The following can generally be omitted from function and method names:</p>

    <ul>
      <li>The types of the inputs and outputs (when there is no collision)</li>
      <li>The type of a method’s receiver</li>
      <li>Whether an input or output is a pointer</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>For functions, do not
<a href="decisions#repetitive-with-package">repeat the name of the package</a>.</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">yamlconfig</span>

<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">ParseYAMLConfig</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">input</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Config</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="kt">error</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Good:</span>
<span class="k">package</span> <span class="n">yamlconfig</span>

<span class="k">func</span> <span class="n">Parse</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">input</span> <span class="kt">string</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="o">*</span><span class="n">Config</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="kt">error</span><span class="p">)</span>
</code></pre></div>    </div>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>For methods, do not repeat the name of the method receiver.</p>

    <div class="language-go highlighter-rouge"><div class="highlight"><pre class="highlight"><code><span class="c">// Bad:</span>
